# OSDC — 2026-09-01

## GHSA-m4rf-3fr8-xwx3 · nltk

2026-09-01 · Python · **CRITICAL** 9.8 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from an incomplete fix for a previous JVM argument injection issue. The `_validate_java_options` function, intended to sanitize JVM arguments, did not adequately restrict per-call options, allowing an attacker to inject dangerous JVM flags like `-XX:OnError` or `-D` system properties. This bypass enabled the execution of arbitrary commands or other malicious actions.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve arbitrary command execution on the system running the NLTK application by injecting specially crafted JVM arguments. This could lead to full system compromise, data exfiltration, or denial of service.

### Key diff

```
--- a/nltk/internals.py
+++ b/nltk/internals.py
@@ -43,23 +59,64 @@
     "-xcomp",  # compile-only mode
     "-xmixed",  # mixed mode (JVM default)
     "-verbose",  # diagnostic output: -verbose:gc
-    "-xx:",  # advanced tuning:  -XX:+UseG1GC
+
 ) 
 
 _SAFE_JVM_EXACT = frozenset({"-server", "-client"})
 
+# ``--add-modules <module-list>`` is required by CoreNLP on JDK 9-11 (a CoreNLP
+# dependency uses the JAXB module dropped from the default set). The value is a
+# comma-separated list of module names -- it names JDK modules, and because
+# ``--module-path`` / ``-p`` is NOT allowlisted it cannot point at attacker code.
+# Restrict the value to a plain module-list shape so nothing else rides through.
+_MODULE_LIST_RE = re.compile(r"\A[A-Za-z0-9_.,-]+\Z")
+
+# Every flag the allowlist accepts (heap/stack sizing, -verbose, -server/-client,
+# --add-modules) is a single simple token; none contains whitespace or a shell
+# metacharacter. Rejecting those characters is therefore a free, name-agnostic
+# defense-in-depth layer (it has no false positives now that -D, whose values may
+# legitimately contain them, is not accepted): e.g. a malformed ``-Xmx512m ; rm``
+# token cannot ride through on the ``-xmx`` prefix.
+_UNSAFE_OPTION_CHARS = frozenset(" \t\r\n;|&$`<>()[]*?!'\"\\")
 
 def _validate_java_options(options):
     """
-    Raise ValueError if *options* contains JVM flags that can change
-    the executed program, load agents, or expand argument files.
-
-    Uses an allowlist of safe JVM memory/tuning flags that NLTK's Java
-    wrapper is known to need.  This is intentionally stricter than a
-    denylist so that -jar, @argfile, and future dangerous flags are
-    rejected without needing to be enumerated (CVE-2026-12841, CWE-88).
+    Raise ValueError if *options* contains JVM flags that can change the
+    executed program, run a command, load agents, or expand argument files.
+
+    Uses a minimal allowlist of exactly the flags NLTK's Java wrappers and the
+    Stanford CoreNLP documentation use (heap/stack sizing, -verbose,
+    -server/-client, and ``--add-modules``). This is intentionally stricter than
+    a denylist so that -jar, @argfile, ``-XX:OnError=<cmd>``, dangerous ``-D``
+    system properties, and future dangerous flags are all rejected without
+    needing to be enumerated (CVE-2026-12841, CWE-88). Applications needing an
+    unlisted flag use ``java(..., trusted_raw_options=[...])``.
     """
-    for flag in options:
+    opts = list(options)
+    i = 0
+    while i < len(opts):
+        flag = opts[i]
+
+        # A JVM flag is a non-empty string; anything else cannot be reasoned
+        # about safely, so reject it rather than call .lower() on it.
+        if not isinstance(flag, str) or not flag:
+            raise ValueError(
+                f"java_options contains an invalid (non-string or empty) entry: "
+                f"{flag!r} (CVE-2026-12841, CWE-88)."
+            )
+
+        # Shape guard: no legitimate allowed flag contains whitespace, a control
+        # character, or a shell metacharacter; reject any that does.
+        if any(
+            c.isspace() or ord(c) < 0x20 or ord(c) == 0x7F or c in _UNSAFE_OPTION_CHARS
+            for c in flag
+        ):
+            raise ValueError(
+                f"java_options contains whitespace, a control character, or a "
+                f"shell metacharacter, which a valid JVM flag never does: "
+                f"{flag!r} (CVE-2026-12841, CWE-88)."
+            )
+
         n = flag.lower()
 
         # @argfile references are expanded by the Java launcher before
@@ -70,21 +127,38 @@ def _validate_java_options(options):
                 f"reference: {flag!r} (CVE-2026-12841, CWE-88)."
             )
 
-        # Allow -Dkey=value system properties. The prefix is always
-        # uppercase -D in valid usage; check the original flag.
-        if flag.startswith("-D") and "=" in flag:
+        # --add-modules <modules>  (two tokens) or  --add-modules=<modules>.
+        if n == "--add-modules":
+            mods = opts[i + 1] if i + 1 < len(opts) else None
+            if not isinstance(mods, str) or not _MODULE_LIST_RE.match(mods):
+                raise ValueError(
+                    f"--add-modules must be followed by a plain module list, got "
+                    f"{mods!r} (CVE-2026-12841, CWE-88)."
+                )
+            i += 2
+            continue
+        if n.startswith("--add-modules="):
+            if not _MODULE_LIST_RE.match(flag.split("=", 1)[1]):
+                raise ValueError(
+                    f"--add-modules has a non-module-list value: {flag!r} "
+                    "(CVE-2026-12841, CWE-88)."
+                )
+            i += 1
             continue
 
         if n in _SAFE_JVM_EXACT:
+            i += 1
             continue
 
         if n.startswith(_SAFE_JVM_PREFIXES):
+            i += 1
             continue
 
         raise ValueError(
             f"java_options contains a disallowed JVM/launcher flag: {flag!r}. "
-            "Only JVM memory-tuning and safe runtime flags are permitted "
-            "(CVE-2026-12841, CWE-88)."
+            "Only JVM memory/stack tuning, -verbose, -server/-client and "
+            "--add-modules are permitted; pass anything else through "
+            "java(trusted_raw_options=...) (CVE-2026-12841, CWE-88)."
         )
 
 
@@ -209,4 +297,10 @@ def java(
         if isinstance(options, str):
             options = options.split()
         java_options = list(options)
+        # Per-call options reach subprocess.Popen directly, so they must be
+        # validated too -- config_java() alone is not enough (CVE-2026-12841,
+        # CWE-88). Without this a caller-supplied -jav
```

### Fix summary

The patch significantly tightens the allowlist for JVM arguments, explicitly removing `-XX:` and `-D` prefixes, which were previously allowed. It also adds new validation checks for unsafe characters and ensures that per-call options are also subjected to the same strict validation as global options, preventing the bypass of the original fix.

## GHSA-gqvg-gmmx-x4hm · mlflow

2026-09-01 · Python · **HIGH** 8.8 · Pattern: `DESERIALIZATION→RCE`

### Root cause

The `mlflow.statsmodels` flavor used `statsmodels.iolib.api.load_pickle` to deserialize models. While MLflow had a general safety control `MLFLOW_ALLOW_PICKLE_DESERIALIZATION=False` to prevent arbitrary code execution via pickle deserialization, this specific flavor bypassed that check, allowing a crafted model artifact to execute arbitrary code.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious model artifact that, when loaded, would execute arbitrary code on the system running the MLflow model, leading to full system compromise.

### Key diff

```
--- a/mlflow/statsmodels/__init__.py
+++ b/mlflow/statsmodels/__init__.py
@@ -305,6 +310,16 @@ def log_model(
 
 
 def _load_model(path):
+    if (
+        not MLFLOW_ALLOW_PICKLE_DESERIALIZATION.get()
+        and not is_in_databricks_runtime()
+        and not is_in_databricks_model_serving_environment()
+    ):
+        raise MlflowException(
+            "Deserializing model using pickle is disallowed, but this model is saved "
+            "in pickle format. The workaround is to set environment variable "
+            "'MLFLOW_ALLOW_PICKLE_DESERIALIZATION' to 'true'."
+        )
     import statsmodels.iolib.api as smio
 
     return smio.load_pickle(path)
```

### Fix summary

The patch adds a check within the `_load_model` function of the `mlflow.statsmodels` flavor. It now explicitly verifies the `MLFLOW_ALLOW_PICKLE_DESERIALIZATION` environment variable and raises an `MlflowException` if deserialization is disallowed, unless the environment is a Databricks runtime or model serving environment.

## GHSA-52xp-w8hr-xv3c · filament/filament

2026-09-01 · PHP · **HIGH** 8.1 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTH→ENDPOINT`

### Root cause

The application's multi-factor authentication (MFA) logic incorrectly handled the 'required' validation for recovery codes. When recovery codes were enabled, the system would not always enforce that a recovery code was actually provided, allowing an attacker to bypass the MFA challenge by submitting an empty recovery code field.

### Impact

An attacker who has compromised a user's primary credentials could bypass the multi-factor authentication requirement and gain unauthorized access to the user's account, even if MFA was enabled.

### Key diff

```
-                    ->required(fn (Get $get): bool => (! $isRecoverable) || (! $get('useRecoveryCode')))
+                    ->required(fn (Get $get): bool => (! $isRecoverable) || (! $get('useRecoveryCode')) || blank($get('recoveryCode')))
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies the 'required' validation rule for the recovery code input field. It now explicitly checks if the 'recoveryCode' field is blank, ensuring that a recovery code must be provided when the 'useRecoveryCode' option is selected and recovery codes are enabled.

## GHSA-6hwm-xvph-95vm · nltk

2026-09-01 · Python · **HIGH** 7.8 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The application invoked external binaries ('dot' from Graphviz) using a bare name (e.g., `["dot", ...]`) instead of its absolute path. This allowed the operating system's PATH environment variable or the current working directory to be used to locate the executable, making it susceptible to an attacker planting a malicious 'dot' binary in a predictable location.

### Impact

An attacker could execute arbitrary code by placing a malicious executable named 'dot' in a directory that is searched before the legitimate Graphviz binary, leading to remote code execution on the system running the NLTK application.

### Key diff

```
--- a/nltk/parse/dependencygraph.py
+++ b/nltk/parse/dependencygraph.py
@@ -582,28 +582,32 @@ def dot2img(dot_string, t="svg"):
     """
 
     try:
-        find_binary("dot")
-        try:
-            if t in ["dot", "dot_json", "json", "svg"]:
-                proc = subprocess.run(
-                    ["dot", "-T%s" % t],
-                    capture_output=True,
-                    input=dot_string,
-                    text=True,
-                )
-            else:
-                proc = subprocess.run(
-                    ["dot", "-T%s" % t],
-                    input=bytes(dot_string, encoding="utf8"),
-                )
-            return proc.stdout
-        except Exception:
-            raise Exception(
-                "Cannot create image representation by running dot from string: {}"
-                "".format(dot_string)
-            )
-    except OSError as e:
+        dot_binary = find_binary("dot")
+    except LookupError as e:
         raise Exception("Cannot find the dot binary from Graphviz package") from e
     try:
         if t in ["dot", "dot_json", "json", "svg"]:
             proc = subprocess.run(
-                ["dot", "-T%s" % t],
+                [dot_binary, "-T%s" % t],
                 capture_output=True,
                 input=dot_string,
                 text=True,
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies the code to explicitly resolve the absolute path of the 'dot' binary using `nltk.internals.find_binary("dot")` before invoking it. This ensures that only the trusted, legitimate Graphviz binary is executed, preventing the use of a planted malicious executable.

## GHSA-73wf-gq98-2v4g · browserslist

2026-09-01 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `PROTOTYPE_POLLUTION→OVERRIDE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because the `normalizeStats` function created objects using `{}`, which inherits from `Object.prototype`. When processing untrusted `stats` data, an attacker could supply a key like `__proto__` to inject or modify properties on `Object.prototype`, affecting all objects in the application.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve denial of service by causing an uncaught crash or potentially manipulate application logic by injecting or overriding properties on `Object.prototype`, leading to unexpected behavior or further vulnerabilities.

### Key diff

```
--- a/node.js
+++ b/node.js
@@ -221,12 +221,13 @@ function normalizeStats(data, stats) {
 
   if (typeof stats !== 'object') return undefined
 
-  var normalized = {}
+  var normalized = Object.create(null)
   for (var i in stats) {
     var versions = Object.keys(stats[i])
-    if (versions.length === 1 && data[i] && data[i].versions.length === 1) {
-      var normal = data[i].versions[0]
-      normalized[i] = {}
+    var known = Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty.call(data, i) && data[i]
+    if (versions.length === 1 && known && known.versions.length === 1) {
+      var normal = known.versions[0]
+      normalized[i] = Object.create(null)
       normalized[i][normal] = stats[i][versions[0]]
     } else {
       normalized[i] = stats[i]
```

### Fix summary

The patch addresses the vulnerability by changing object creation from `{}` to `Object.create(null)`. This ensures that newly created objects do not inherit from `Object.prototype`, preventing an attacker from polluting the prototype chain via untrusted input.

## GHSA-8rr7-cvq3-gmfh · league/commonmark

2026-09-01 · PHP · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from an inefficient merging strategy for attributes, specifically the 'class' attribute, within the CommonMark parser. When multiple lines contributed distinct attributes, especially 'class' attributes, the previous implementation would re-split and re-join the entire class list on every merge operation. This led to a quadratic time complexity (O(N^2)) for documents with many lines adding attributes, causing excessive CPU usage and memory consumption.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious Markdown document with a large number of lines, each adding distinct attributes. Parsing such a document would consume disproportionately high CPU and memory resources, leading to a denial of service for applications processing user-supplied Markdown.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/Extension/Attributes/Parser/AttributesBlockContinueParser.php
+++ b/src/Extension/Attributes/Parser/AttributesBlockContinueParser.php
-            $merged = AttributesHelper::mergeAttributes(
-                $this->block->getAttributes(),
-                $attributes
-            );
+            $this->classFirst = $this->classFirst || $this->hasClass;
 
-            if (isset($merged['class'])) {
-                if ($this->class === null) {
-                    $this->class = $merged['class'];
-                } else {
-                    // Only this line's own classes can have been added to the list, so record them
-                    // and put the short value the merge started from back in their place.
-                    foreach (AttributesHelper::classList($attributes['class'] ?? []) as $class) {
-                        $this->additionalClasses[] = $class;
-                    }
-
-                    $merged['class'] = $this->class;
-                }
-            }
-
-            $this->block->setAttributes($merged);
+            $this->absorb($attributes);
```

### Fix summary

The patch refactors the attribute merging logic to accumulate attributes, particularly 'class' attributes, in a linear fashion. Instead of re-merging the entire list on each line, it now stores individual classes in a list and merges other attributes into a dictionary. The final attribute set is constructed only once at the end, avoiding repeated expensive operations and reducing complexity to O(N).

## GHSA-c83g-rgw3-j3cx · browserslist

2026-09-01 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The application used unbounded caches (plain JavaScript objects) to store results of browserlist queries and parsing. Over time, as distinct queries were made, these caches would grow indefinitely without any eviction mechanism, consuming more and more memory.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a large number of unique queries, causing the application's memory usage to continuously increase until it exhausts available memory, leading to an Out-of-Memory (OOM) error and application crash, effectively denying service.

### Key diff

```
--- var cache = {}
--- var parseCache = {}
+++ var CACHE_MAX_ENTRIES = 500
+++ var cache = new Map()
+++ var parseCache = new Map()
```

### Fix summary

The patch replaces the unbounded JavaScript object caches with `Map` objects and introduces a `boundedCacheSet` function. This function ensures that the caches do not exceed a maximum of 500 entries by evicting the oldest entry when the limit is reached before adding a new one.

## GHSA-j8pm-gj4c-rq4x · league/commonmark

2026-09-01 · PHP · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because the parser did not properly limit the length of reference labels within square brackets. Crafting an input with deeply-nested or excessively long bracket sequences could lead to quadratic processing time, consuming excessive CPU resources.

### Impact

An attacker could provide specially crafted input to the application, causing the server to consume excessive CPU resources and potentially become unresponsive, leading to a denial of service for legitimate users.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/Extension/CommonMark/Parser/Inline/CloseBracketParser.php
+++ b/src/Extension/CommonMark/Parser/Inline/CloseBracketParser.php
@@ -181,6 +181,16 @@ private function tryParseReference(Cursor $cursor, ReferenceMapInterface $refere
             // The reference must not contain a bracket. If we know there's a bracket, we don't even bother checking it.
             $start  = $opener->getPosition();
             $length = $startPos - $start;
+
+            // spec: A link label can have at most 999 characters inside the square brackets.
+            // ReferenceParser::parseLabel() enforces that when storing definitions, so a longer
+            // span here cannot possibly match one. Rejecting it up-front - before copying and
+            // normalizing the span - is what keeps deeply-nested brackets from being quadratic.
+            if ($length > 999) {
+                $cursor->restoreState($savePos);
+
+                return null;
+            }
         } else {
             $cursor->restoreState($savePos);
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a length check for reference labels, ensuring that any label exceeding 999 characters is immediately rejected. This prevents the parser from attempting to process excessively long or deeply-nested bracket sequences, thereby mitigating the quadratic complexity issue.

## GHSA-jjv6-8j6v-6j52 · league/commonmark

2026-09-01 · PHP · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because the `AdjacentTextMerger` class, when merging multiple adjacent `Text` nodes, repeatedly concatenated strings using the `.` operator in a loop. This operation has a quadratic time complexity (O(n^2)) for string concatenation in PHP when the string grows, leading to performance degradation with a large number of small text nodes.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious input that, when parsed by the `league/commonmark` library, would cause the application to consume excessive CPU resources and time, leading to a denial of service for legitimate users.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/Node/Inline/AdjacentTextMerger.php
+++ b/src/Node/Inline/AdjacentTextMerger.php
@@ -89,17 +89,13 @@ private static function mergeIfNeeded(?Text $first, ?Text $last): void
             return;
         }
 
-        $s = $first->getLiteral();
-
         $node = $first->next();
         $stop = $last->next();
         while ($node !== $stop && $node instanceof Text) {
-            $s     .= $node->getLiteral();
+            $first->append($node->getLiteral());
             $unlink = $node;
             $node   = $node->next();
             $unlink->detach();
         }
-
-        $first->setLiteral($s);
     }
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies the `mergeIfNeeded` method to use the `append` method of the `Text` node directly, which likely handles string concatenation more efficiently (e.g., by appending to an internal buffer or using a more optimized string building approach) instead of repeatedly reallocating and copying strings with the `.` operator.

## GHSA-vx52-2968-3vc6 · pnpm

2026-09-01 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.4 · Pattern: `INFO_DISCLOSURE→ERROR_MESSAGE`

### Root cause

The pnpm package manager was vulnerable to environment variable exfiltration. It would expand environment variable placeholders (e.g., ${VAR}) in certain configuration settings read from an untrusted pnpm-workspace.yaml file. Specifically, proxy-related settings were not included in the list of keys that should prevent such expansion, allowing an attacker to craft a malicious workspace file to exfiltrate sensitive environment variables via network requests.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious pnpm-workspace.yaml file that, when processed by pnpm, would cause sensitive environment variables (like API keys or tokens) to be included in network requests (e.g., proxy URLs) made by pnpm, thereby exfiltrating them to an attacker-controlled server.

### Key diff

```
--- a/config/config/src/getOptionsFromRootManifest.ts
+++ b/config/config/src/getOptionsFromRootManifest.ts
@@ -120,7 +120,7 @@ export function getOptionsFromPnpmSettings (manifestDir: string, pnpmSettings: P
 // root manifest are repository-controlled, so expanding ${...} placeholders
 // in these values would let a repository exfiltrate environment variables
 // through the URLs pnpm sends requests to.
-const REQUEST_DESTINATION_SCALAR_KEYS = new Set(['registry'])
+const REQUEST_DESTINATION_SCALAR_KEYS = new Set(['registry', 'httpProxy', 'httpsProxy', 'noProxy', 'proxy', 'noproxy'])
```

### Fix summary

The patch extends the set of configuration keys for which environment variable expansion is disabled when reading from untrusted root manifests. It adds 'httpProxy', 'httpsProxy', 'noProxy', 'proxy', and 'noproxy' to the `REQUEST_DESTINATION_SCALAR_KEYS` set, preventing the expansion of ${...} placeholders in these sensitive proxy-related settings.

## GHSA-xwg4-73v4-xw9w · nanoid

2026-09-01 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.4 · Pattern: `INTEGER_OVERFLOW→BOUNDARY`

### Root cause

The `fillPool` function, responsible for allocating a buffer for random bytes, did not properly validate the `bytes` parameter. While it checked for negative values, it did not set an upper bound, allowing an attacker to provide a very large integer. This large integer, when multiplied by `POOL_SIZE_MULTIPLIER`, could lead to an integer overflow, resulting in a much smaller buffer allocation than intended.

### Impact

An attacker could cause a denial of service by triggering an integer overflow, leading to an undersized buffer. This could potentially cause subsequent memory access issues or crashes when the application attempts to write more data than the allocated buffer size.

### Key diff

```
--- a/index.js
+++ b/index.js
@@ -13,7 +13,7 @@ const POOL_SIZE_MULTIPLIER = 128
 let pool, poolOffset
 
 function fillPool(bytes) {
-  if (bytes < 0) bytes = 0
+  if (bytes < 0 || bytes > 1024) throw new RangeError('Wrong ID size')
```

### Fix summary

The patch adds an upper bound check to the `bytes` parameter in the `fillPool` function. If `bytes` is greater than 1024, a `RangeError` is thrown, preventing excessively large or malicious values from causing an integer overflow during buffer allocation.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 17 occurrences**
> Languages: Go, C#, RUST, PHP, Python, JavaScript

## GHSA-f8fg-pg57-v4j8 · league/commonmark

2026-09-01 · PHP · **HIGH** 7.2 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→XSS`

### Root cause

The `league/commonmark` library's `AttributesExtension` used PHP's default `trim()` function, which does not remove the U+000C (form feed) character. An attacker could embed this character within an attribute name, specifically `on*` event handlers, allowing it to bypass the XSS filter. Browsers would then interpret the form feed as whitespace, effectively rendering the malicious event handler.

### Impact

An attacker could inject arbitrary JavaScript code into the rendered HTML, leading to Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) attacks. This could allow them to steal user sessions, deface the website, or redirect users to malicious sites.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/Extension/Attributes/Util/AttributesHelper.php
+++ b/src/Extension/Attributes/Util/AttributesHelper.php
@@ -26,6 +26,13 @@ final class AttributesHelper
     private const SINGLE_ATTRIBUTE = '\s*([.]-?[_a-z][^\s.}]*|[#][^\s}]+|' . RegexHelper::PARTIAL_ATTRIBUTENAME . RegexHelper::PARTIAL_ATTRIBUTEVALUESPEC . ')\s*';
     private const ATTRIBUTE_LIST   = '/^{:?(' . self::SINGLE_ATTRIBUTE . ')+}/i';
 
+    /**
+     * PCRE's `\s` matches the form feed that PHP's default trim charlist omits, so the
+     * separators SINGLE_ATTRIBUTE accepts must be trimmed with this list instead - otherwise
+     * that byte survives inside an attribute name, where a browser reads it as a separator.
+     */
+    private const WHITESPACE = " \t\n\r\0\x0B\x0C";
+
     /**
      * @return array<string, mixed>
      */
@@ -59,7 +66,7 @@ public static function parseAttributes(Cursor $cursor): array
 
         /** @var array<string, mixed> $attributes */
         $attributes = [];
-        while ($attribute = \trim((string) $attributeCursor->match('/^' . self::SINGLE_ATTRIBUTE . '/i'))) {
+        while ($attribute = \trim((string) $attributeCursor->match('/^' . self::SINGLE_ATTRIBUTE . '/i'), self::WHITESPACE)) {
             if ($attribute[0] === '#') {
                 $attributes['id'] = \substr($attribute, 1);
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a custom `WHITESPACE` constant that explicitly includes the U+000C form feed character. This custom constant is then used in all `trim()` operations within the `parseAttributes` method to ensure that form feed characters are properly removed from attribute names and values. Additionally, a new regex check is added to `filterAttributes` to ensure attribute names are well-formed.

## GHSA-2rx9-3g3h-c2jv · pnpm

2026-09-01 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.1 · Pattern: `PATH_TRAVERSAL→FILE_WRITE`

### Root cause

The package manager, `pacquet`, did not adequately validate package names and versions when constructing paths for symlinks in the virtual store. Specifically, when `enable_global_virtual_store` was true, the version segment was inserted as a raw path component, allowing path traversal sequences (e.g., `../../../`) in the version string to escape the intended virtual store directory. Similarly, dependency aliases containing path traversal sequences were not properly rejected before symlink creation.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious `PackageKey` or dependency alias in a lockfile that, when processed by `pnpm` (which uses `pacquet`), would cause symlinks to be created outside the project's intended virtual store. This could lead to arbitrary file writes or overwrites on the system where the installation is performed, potentially allowing for remote code execution or other system compromise.

### Key diff

```
--- a/pacquet/crates/package-manager/src/validate_lockfile_paths.rs
+++ b/pacquet/crates/package-manager/src/validate_lockfile_paths.rs
@@ -0,0 +1,52 @@
+pub fn validate_virtual_store_slot_containment(
+    snapshots: Option<&HashMap<PackageKey, SnapshotEntry>>,
+    layout: &VirtualStoreLayout,
+) -> Result<(), VerifyError> {
+    let Some(snapshots) = snapshots else {
+        return Ok(());
+    };
+    let mut escaped: BTreeSet<String> = BTreeSet::new();
+    for key in snapshots.keys() {
+        if !is_subdir(layout.package_store_dir(), &layout.slot_dir(key)) {
+            escaped.insert(key.to_string());
+        }
+    }
+    if escaped.is_empty() {
+        return Ok(());
+    }
+    let escaped: Vec<String> = escaped.into_iter().collect();
+    Err(VerifyError::invalid_dependency_aliases(&escaped))
+}
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces `validate_virtual_store_slot_containment` to explicitly check that all computed virtual-store slot paths remain within the store root. It also adds a check in `create_symlink_layout` to reject dependency aliases that contain path traversal sequences. These checks prevent the creation of symlinks that point outside the designated virtual store directories.

## GHSA-23w6-3w8w-8484 · pypdf

2026-09-01 · Python · **MODERATE** 0.0 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The pypdf library did not limit the depth or number of entries when parsing PDF outlines or extracting text from XObjects. Maliciously crafted PDF files with deeply nested or excessively numerous outline entries, or recursive XObject references, could lead to unbounded recursion or iteration.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious PDF file that, when processed by pypdf, would cause the application to consume excessive CPU time and memory, leading to a denial of service for the application or system processing the PDF.

### Key diff

```
--- a/pypdf/_doc_common.py
+++ b/pypdf/_doc_common.py
@@ -894,6 +905,9 @@ def _get_outline(
         if node is None:
             return outline
 
+        if depth > OUTLINE_MAX_DEPTH:
+            raise LimitReachedError(f"Maximum outline depth reached: {depth} > {OUTLINE_MAX_DEPTH}.")
+
         # see if there are any more outline items
         if visited is None:
             visited = set()
--- a/pypdf/_page.py
+++ b/pypdf/_page.py
@@ -1913,31 +1920,19 @@ def _extract_text(
                 except IndexError:
                     pass
                 try:
-                    xobj = cast(DictionaryObject, resources_dict["/XObject"])
-                    xform = cast(EncodedStreamObject, xobj[operands[0]])
-                    if xform["/Subtype"] != NameObject("/Image"):
-                        xform_id = id(xform)
-                        if xform_id in known_ids:
-                            logger_warning(
-                                "Detected cyclic form XObject reference, skipping %(operand)s.",
-                                source=__name__,
-                                operand=operands[0]
-                            )
-                            text = ""
-                        else:
-                            known_ids.add(xform_id)
-                            try:
-                                text = self.extract_xform_text(
-                                    xform,
-                                    orientations,
-                                    space_width,
-                                    visitor_operand_before,
-                                    visitor_operand_after,
-                                    visitor_text,
-                                    known_ids=known_ids,
-                                )
-                            finally:
-                                known_ids.discard(xform_id)
+                    xform_text = self._extract_text__xform(
+                        resources_dict=resources_dict,
+                        operands=operands,
+                        orientations=orientations,
+                        space_width=space_width,
+                        visitor_operand_before=visitor_operand_before,
+                        visitor_operand_after=visitor_operand_after,
+                        visitor_text=visitor_text,
+                        known_ids=known_ids,
+                        traversal_state=traversal_state
+                    )
+                    if xform_text is not None:
+                        text = xform_text
                         extractor.output += text
                         if visitor_text is not None:
                             visitor_text(
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces maximum limits for outline depth (OUTLINE_MAX_DEPTH) and outline entries (OUTLINE_MAX_ENTRIES), as well as for XForm invocations during text extraction (MAX_XFORM_INVOCATIONS_PER_EXTRACTION). It also adds a _TraversalState object to track the current depth and entry count during recursive operations, raising a LimitReachedError if these limits are exceeded.

## GHSA-3f6p-5ww8-9rcr · mysql2

2026-09-01 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `MISSING_CRYPTO→PLAINTEXT`

### Root cause

The `mysql2` library, by default, did not explicitly disable the `mysql_clear_password` authentication plugin. This allowed a malicious or compromised MySQL server to request a downgrade to this plugin, which transmits the user's password in plaintext, without requiring explicit user consent or configuration.

### Impact

An attacker controlling the MySQL server or an intermediary could force the client to send credentials in plaintext, allowing them to intercept and steal the user's database password.

### Key diff

```
--- a/lib/commands/auth_switch.js
+++ b/lib/commands/auth_switch.js
@@ -72,6 +72,28 @@ function authSwitchRequest(packet, connection, command) {
     return;
   }
 
+  if (pluginName === 'mysql_clear_password') {
+    const hasCustomPlugin =
+      connection.config.authPlugins &&
+      Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty.call(
+        connection.config.authPlugins,
+        'mysql_clear_password'
+      );
+    if (!hasCustomPlugin && !connection.config.enableCleartextPlugin) {
+      const err = new Error(
+        'Server requested authentication using mysql_clear_password, ' +
+          'which sends the password in plaintext over the network and is ' +
+          'disabled by default. To enable it, set the `enableCleartextPlugin` ' +
+          'option to `true` in your connection configuration, or provide a ' +
+          'custom `mysql_clear_password` auth plugin via the `authPlugins` ' +
+          'option. Only use this over a secure connection (TLS/SSL).'
+      );
+      err.code = 'MYSQL_CLEAR_PASSWORD_NOT_ENABLED';
+      err.fatal = true;
+      throw err;
+    }
+  }
+
   const authPlugin = getAuthPlugin(pluginName, connection);
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces an `enableCleartextPlugin` option, which is `false` by default. It now explicitly checks this flag before allowing the `mysql_clear_password` plugin to be used, preventing its use unless explicitly enabled by the developer. An error is thrown if the server requests this plugin without it being enabled.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 4 occurrences**
> Languages: PHP, Go, JavaScript
> → GHSA-xvv6-p4wf-mvx7
> → GHSA-q4vm-pq3q-8wgq
> → GHSA-h4mf-4v27-hggj

## GHSA-68jx-f42c-7599 · typo3/cms-core

2026-09-01 · PHP · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `CSRF→STATE_CHANGE`

### Root cause

The `ReferrerEnforcer` class in TYPO3 CMS incorrectly determined 'same-origin' by checking if the referrer URL started with the request directory. Since both the backend and install tool shared the same entry script directory, a referrer from the backend was mistakenly considered 'same-origin' for the install tool, and vice-versa. This allowed an attacker to bypass referrer checks designed to prevent CSRF.

### Impact

An attacker could potentially bypass CSRF protections in the TYPO3 backend or install tool, leading to unauthorized actions if they could trick an authenticated user into clicking a malicious link.

### Key diff

```
--- a/typo3/sysext/core/Classes/Http/Security/ReferrerEnforcer.php
+++ b/typo3/sysext/core/Classes/Http/Security/ReferrerEnforcer.php
-class ReferrerEnforcer
+abstract class ReferrerEnforcer
 ...
-    protected function resolveReferrerType(): int
-    {
-        $referrer = $this->request->getServerParams()['HTTP_REFERER'] ?? '';
-        if ($referrer === '') {
-            return self::TYPE_REFERRER_EMPTY;
-        }
-        if (str_starts_with($referrer, $this->requestDir)) {
-            // same-origin implies same-site
-            return self::TYPE_REFERRER_SAME_ORIGIN | self::TYPE_REFERRER_SAME_SITE;
-        }
-        if (str_starts_with($referrer, $this->requestHost)) {
-            return self::TYPE_REFERRER_SAME_SITE;
-        }
-        return 0;
-    }
+    abstract protected function resolveReferrerType(): int;
```

### Fix summary

The `ReferrerEnforcer` class was made abstract, and its `resolveReferrerType` method was moved to concrete implementations for the backend and install tool. These new implementations now correctly determine 'same-origin' by specifically checking against the respective application's entry script (e.g., `typo3/install.php` for the install tool) instead of just the shared directory.

## GHSA-763m-79hh-57f2 · pypdf

2026-09-01 · Python · **MODERATE** 0.0 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The pypdf library did not properly limit the depth of outline structures or the number of XForm object invocations during text extraction. This allowed specially crafted PDF files with deeply nested outlines or recursive XForm references to cause excessive memory consumption and long processing times, leading to a denial of service.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious PDF file that, when processed by a vulnerable application using pypdf, would consume excessive CPU and memory resources, potentially crashing the application or making it unresponsive.

### Key diff

```
--- a/pypdf/_doc_common.py
+++ b/pypdf/_doc_common.py
@@ -894,6 +905,9 @@ def _get_outline(
         if node is None:
             return outline
 
+        if depth > OUTLINE_MAX_DEPTH:
+            raise LimitReachedError(f"Maximum outline depth reached: {depth} > {OUTLINE_MAX_DEPTH}.")
+
         # see if there are any more outline items
         if visited is None:
             visited = set()
--- a/pypdf/_page.py
+++ b/pypdf/_page.py
@@ -1913,31 +1920,19 @@ def _extract_text(
                 except IndexError:
                     pass
                 try:
-                    xobj = cast(DictionaryObject, resources_dict["/XObject"])
-                    xform = cast(EncodedStreamObject, xobj[operands[0]])
-                    if xform["/Subtype"] != NameObject("/Image"):
-                        xform_id = id(xform)
-                        if xform_id in known_ids:
-                            logger_warning(
-                                "Detected cyclic form XObject reference, skipping %(operand)s.",
-                                source=__name__,
-                                operand=operands[0]
-                            )
-                            text = ""
-                        else:
-                            known_ids.add(xform_id)
-                            try:
-                                text = self.extract_xform_text(
-                                    xform,
-                                    orientations,
-                                    space_width,
-                                    visitor_operand_before,
-                                    visitor_operand_after,
-                                    visitor_text,
-                                    known_ids=known_ids,
-                                )
-                            finally:
-                                known_ids.discard(xform_id)
+                    xform_text = self._extract_text__xform(
+                        resources_dict=resources_dict,
+                        operands=operands,
+                        orientations=orientations,
+                        space_width=space_width,
+                        visitor_operand_before=visitor_operand_before,
+                        visitor_operand_after=visitor_operand_after,
+                        visitor_text=visitor_text,
+                        known_ids=known_ids,
+                        traversal_state=traversal_state
+                    )
+                    if xform_text is not None:
+                        text = xform_text
                         extractor.output += text
                         if visitor_text is not None:
                             visitor_text(
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces `OUTLINE_MAX_ENTRIES`, `OUTLINE_MAX_DEPTH`, and `MAX_XFORM_INVOCATIONS_PER_EXTRACTION` limits. It adds a `_TraversalState` object to track the number of entries and depth during outline parsing and XForm text extraction, raising a `LimitReachedError` if these thresholds are exceeded.

## GHSA-cfqr-cjx5-5jcm · sqlparse

2026-09-01 · Python · **MODERATE** 0.0 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The `_get_offset` method in `ReindentFilter` previously rebuilt the statement prefix from the start of the statement for every call. When reindenting a list of N parenthesized tuples or identifier lists, this led to an O(N^2) complexity, causing near-quadratic CPU consumption.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a SQL query with a large number of tuples (e.g., in `IN (...)` or `VALUES` clauses) to trigger this quadratic complexity, leading to high CPU usage and a denial of service for the application processing the SQL.

### Key diff

```
--- a/sqlparse/filters/reindent.py
+++ b/sqlparse/filters/reindent.py
@@ -27,25 +27,65 @@ def __init__(self, width=2, char=' ', wrap_after=0, n='\n',
         self._last_stmt = None
         self._last_func = None
 
-    def _flatten_up_to_token(self, token):
-        """Yields all tokens up to token but excluding current."""
-        if token.is_group:
-            token = next(token.flatten())
-
-        for t in self._curr_stmt.flatten():
-            if t == token:
-                break
-            yield t
-
     @property
     def leading_ws(self):
         return self.offset + self.indent * self.width
 
+    def _current_line_len(self, token):
+        """Returns the width of what's already emitted on *token*'s line.
+
+        The tokens preceding *token* are visited last one first, so the walk
+        stops at the line break that starts the current line.  Rebuilding the
+        statement prefix from its start instead made every caller
+        O(statement), and the callers running once per group (tuple lists,
+        identifier lists) quadratic in the number of groups -- a CPU
+        exhaustion vector (GHSA-cfqr-cjx5-5jcm).  The walk is inlined and
+        counts characters rather than collecting them: both matter, since a
+        line without any break still has to be measured token by token.
+        """
+        length = 0
+        node = token
+        while node is not self._curr_stmt and node.parent is not None:
+            parent = node.parent
+            # ``Token`` doesn't implement ``__eq__``, so ``index()`` is an
+            # identity lookup and safe against tokens sharing a value.
+            stack = parent.tokens[:parent.tokens.index(node)]
+            while stack:
+                prev_ = stack.pop()
+                if prev_.is_group:
+                    stack.extend(prev_.tokens)
+                    continue
+
+                value = prev_.value
+                size = len(value)
+                if not size:
+                    continue
+                lines = value.splitlines()
+                if len(lines) == 1 and len(lines[0]) == size:
+                    # No break in here.  ``splitlines()`` hands back the value
+                    # itself in that case, so this costs a scan but no copy --
+                    # which is what keeps a long break-free line affordable.
+                    length += size
+                    continue
+
+                # ``value`` holds the break that starts the current line.  The
+                # sentinel keeps a trailing break from collapsing, so ``lines``
+                # always has one entry more than the number of breaks.
+                lines = (value + '.').splitlines()
+                tail = len(lines[-1]) - 1 + length
+                if tail:
+                    return tail
+                # Nothing but a break to our right, and ``splitlines()`` drops
+                # that empty line -- so the line to measure is the one before.
+                if len(lines) > 2:
+                    return len(lines[-2])
+                length = len(lines[0])
+            node = parent
+        return length
+
     def _get_offset(self, token):
-        raw = ''.join(map(str, self._flatten_up_to_token(token)))
-        line = (raw or '\n').splitlines()[-1]
         # Now take current offset into account and return relative offset.
-        return len(line) - len(self.char * self.leading_ws)
+        return self._current_line_len(token) - len(self.char * self.leading_ws)
 
     def nl(self, offset=0):
         return sql.Token(
```

### Fix summary

The patch refactors the `_get_offset` method to `_current_line_len`, which now calculates the line length by walking backward from the current token to the start of the line. This avoids rebuilding the entire statement prefix, changing the complexity from quadratic to linear.

## GHSA-cq55-c7wv-pxmq · smarty/smarty

2026-09-01 · PHP · **MODERATE** 0.0 · Pattern: `SSRF→INTERNAL_ACCESS`

### Root cause

The `file_get_contents()` function, when used with remote URLs, follows HTTP redirects by default. The existing security policy only validated the initial URL, allowing an attacker to bypass the trusted URI check if an open redirect existed on an otherwise trusted host. This could then lead to the `file_get_contents()` call fetching content from an untrusted, internal, or sensitive target.

### Impact

An attacker could leverage an open redirect on a trusted domain to make the server perform requests to arbitrary internal or external resources, potentially leading to information disclosure, port scanning, or interaction with internal services.

### Key diff

```
-			$content = @file_get_contents($params['file']);
+				$context = stream_context_create([
+					'http' => [
+						'follow_location' => 0,
+						'max_redirects' => 1,
+					],
+				]);
+				$content = @file_get_contents($params['file'], false, $context);
```

### Fix summary

The patch disables HTTP redirect following for `file_get_contents()` when a security policy is active. It explicitly sets `follow_location` to `0` and `max_redirects` to `1` within a stream context, preventing the function from automatically redirecting to untrusted locations after an initial trusted URI check.

## GHSA-jp53-mhqp-8xcg · pypdf

2026-09-01 · Python · **MODERATE** 0.0 · Pattern: `DOS→INFINITE_LOOP`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because the `insert_child` method, when traversing a tree structure to find an insertion point, did not detect cycles. If a malformed PDF document contained a cyclical reference in the tree, the `while` loop would run indefinitely.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious PDF file that, when processed by the pypdf library, would cause an infinite loop, leading to a denial of service for any application or service parsing the document.

### Key diff

```
--- a/pypdf/generic/_data_structures.py
+++ b/pypdf/generic/_data_structures.py
@@ -817,18 +817,25 @@ def insert_child(
             return child_reference
         prev = cast("DictionaryObject", self["/Last"])
 
+        visited: set[int] = set()
         while prev.indirect_reference != before:
+            prev_id = id(prev)
+            if prev_id in visited:
+                raise LimitReachedError("Detected cycle in tree structure.")
+            visited.add(prev_id)
             if "/Next" in prev:
                 prev = cast("TreeObject", prev["/Next"])
-            else:  # append at the end
-                prev[NameObject("/Next")] = cast("TreeObject", child_reference)
-                child_obj[NameObject("/Prev")] = prev.indirect_reference
-                child_obj[NameObject("/Parent")] = self.indirect_reference
-                if "/Next" in child_obj:
-                    del child_obj["/Next"]
-                self[NameObject("/Last")] = child_reference
-                inc_parent_counter(self, child_obj.get("/Count", 1))
-                return child_reference
+                continue
+
+            # append at the end
+            prev[NameObject("/Next")] = cast("TreeObject", child_reference)
+            child_obj[NameObject("/Prev")] = prev.indirect_reference
+            child_obj[NameObject("/Parent")] = self.indirect_reference
+            if "/Next" in child_obj:
+                del child_obj["/Next"]
+            self[NameObject("/Last")] = child_reference
+            inc_parent_counter(self, child_obj.get("/Count", 1))
+            return child_reference
         try:  # insert as first or in the middle
             assert isinstance(prev["/Prev"], DictionaryObject)
             prev["/Prev"][NameObject("/Next")] = child_reference
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a `visited` set to track `prev` objects encountered during the loop. Before processing `prev`, its ID is added to the set. If an ID is encountered that is already in the set, it indicates a cycle, and a `LimitReachedError` is raised, preventing the infinite loop.
